# Review — 041-task-pruning

## Summary

041's first review of its reopening. The window runs from `734b427c`, the reopen's parent, to `5e00b87b`. Two agents ran all five passes over halves of the window, each reading all nine loaded backend and cross rule files whole. They read 25 of the 52 scope entries whole: the contract (spec, plan, data model, tasks and all seven scenarios, each scenario checked claim by claim), the task-counter, prune-tasks, mark-task, read-tasks, label-criteria and prune-project modules, the prune command, its reference, the clarify and specify references, the label-backfill migration and four mutation lists. In depth they reviewed the commits no committed review had read: tasks 22 and 24 (`942b3967`, `552633d4`, `0a7787b4`), task 25 (`ef7ecf3b`), `fbb62dfe`, `314e7571` and `b1d319f1`'s `primitives.rs` hunk; `2f61d67d` and `ab5eaa49` (075's review), `2f6be508` (035's and 079's) and the commits naming no spec were read by committed reviews and not re-reviewed. The other entries are unread: the five generated copies were checked against their sources, the CHANGELOG, append-task, check-artifacts, mod.rs, the two schema files, mcp.rs and the analyze reference were read at the window's hunks, and the entries only already-read commits touched were not opened. Fixed in the run: three contract breaks (a task numbered `u32::MAX` made `prune-tasks` write a `next-task: 0` its own reader refuses, and `append-task` had the same wrap; a `#` comment in `tasks.md`'s frontmatter was taken as the H1 by `--reset`, and keep-pending inserted a line after the opening fence; AC15, the plan and a scenario still routed project-only knowledge to AGENTS.md against 075's routing that `/prune` step 8 applies), the QUAL-DELEG-001 finding (a signed number is now pinned as no task), and 19 chores to stale spec, plan, data-model, scenario and doc-comment claims and missing tests. QUAL-TEST-001 stays open at low confidence: no deterministic test can reach the apply's re-check of a rewritten file, since the read refuses the same path first. Seven observations were waived by the rule, nine stored waivers still stand and are carried, and six expired.

2 fix round(s): 2 re-read, 0 diff-checked, 0 exempt, 0 not re-read.

## MUST violations (blocking)

*None.*

## SHOULD violations (advisory)

*None.*

## Low-confidence findings

### LOW-CONFIDENCE: QUAL-TEST-001 — No test fails when the apply's rewrite stops going through write_contained

- **File**: `runtime/src/primitives/prune_project.rs:132-135, 1338-1352`
- **Rule**: A test SHOULD fail when the behavior it names is removed or broken. A test that passes whether or not the code under test behaves as named SHOULD NOT be added or counted as coverage.
- **Finding**: ef7ecf3b routes the apply's rewrite through `write_contained(repo, rel, ..)` in `run`, but `a_rewrite_is_refused_through_a_link_leaving_the_project` calls the helper directly and mutant M2 mutates the helper's body only. Replacing the call at line 135 with `write_atomic(&repo.join(rel), ..)` leaves all six new tests and every older one green, because `read_subject` already keeps a file behind an outward link out of `contents`, so no apply reaches a rewrite through one. The behavior data-model.md states (an apply writes each changed file after re-checking its path) is pinned at the helper, not at the call that matters. Low confidence: the window the re-check closes (a link swapped between the read and the write) cannot be raced from a test without a seam, so a waiver naming that may be the right answer.
- **Auto-fixable**: no

## Waived findings

*None.*

## Deferred findings

*None.*

## Observations

- bug — a tasks.md holding a task numbered 4294967295 (u32::MAX) makes `prune-tasks --apply` write `next-task: 0`: `highest_present(content) + 1` (prune_tasks.rs reduce, `past`) and `next_number + 1` (append_task.rs run, counter advance) wrap in release (runtime/Cargo.toml [profile.release] sets no overflow-checks), and tasks_counter::read rejects 0, so every later prune-tasks and append-task on that file fails with malformed-tasks. Reproduced on the built binary with a fixture (`## 4294967295.` spent + `## 2.` pending): the file came back with `next-task: 0` and both primitives then refused it. Low impact (typo or hostile input), but the primitive writes a counter its own reader refuses, so the scenario's guarantee that appends keep working and never reissue a number fails for it. — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_tasks.rs:350` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed**
- bug — a `#` YAML comment inside tasks.md's frontmatter is read as the file's H1: `--reset` takes the first line opening with `#` and a space anywhere in the file (reduce_reset scans every block's lines, frontmatter included) as the feature identity, so a block `---\n# owner note\nnext-task: 3\n---\n\n# Real Title` resets to the frontmatter followed by `# owner note` and the body, and the real title is lost; keep-pending's segment() does the same to its first block and joins a blank line after the opening `---`. Reproduced on the built binary (fixtures K and K2). The frontmatter is new to tasks.md in this window, and `set` keeps every other line of an existing block, so a hand-added comment survives to meet this. — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_tasks.rs:573` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed**
- other — claim: the pruned-number work left four statements describing the file before the frontmatter block. spec.md Behavior (line 28) says a pruned file `starts with the template heading` and AC4 (line 51) says it `starts with the template's top-level heading`, AC6 (line 53) says a reset gives `heading plus guidance comment`, and data-model.md §reset (lines 115-138) draws the reset output as the H1 and the body only; a prune that removes a section now writes `---\nnext-task: N\n---` above the heading (CHANGELOG: `such a prune's output begins with ---`). 942b3967 amended AC13 and added AC21 but not these. framework/reference/prune.md (half B's) says a reset `keeps the file's # … heading and replaces everything below it`, which omits the block above. — `specs/041-task-pruning/spec.md:51` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — claim: plan.md records no design for task 24. Its Affected Files table has no row for runtime/src/primitives/mark_task.rs, read_tasks.rs, check_artifacts.rs, framework/reference/analyze.md or scripts/mutation/suffixed-task-number.py, which 0a7787b4 changed or created, and describes mod.rs and append_task.rs by their earlier purposes; no Technical Decision states the suffixed-number grammar (`split_numbered_heading` as its one home, `TaskNumber`'s order, `append-task` numbering past the highest digits and never writing a suffix, `task-number` a string), and `Reuse the tasks.md parser — no new grammar` reads against it. The scenario holds the requirement, but the plan is the design record. — `specs/041-task-pruning/plan.md:411` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — claim: the suffixed-number scenario's Context says in the present tense that this repository holds it only in 023, as `### 1b.`, `### 7b.` and `### 12b.`, all ticked. - other — claim: the pruned-number scenario's Resolved Questions records `928 of 3,643 commit subjects and 953 mentions in 186 spec files outside tasks.md` with no method. - convention — doc comments state the behavior before the counter and the suffix grammar: prune_tasks.rs module doc (lines 9-12) says a reset writes `the existing # … heading followed by` the body, `reduce_reset`'s doc (line 563) says `existing H1 + [CANONICAL_EMPTY_TASKS_BODY]`, and `run`'s `# Errors` (lines 114-115) lists MalformedTasks for a `--reset` file with no H1 only, while a keep-pending call now errors on a frontmatter block that never closes or a counter that is not a positive integer; mod.rs:3301 says a numeric remnant is a heading whose text begins with `N.` (`decimal digits, then dot`), which now includes one lowercase letter. — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_tasks.rs:9` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — test: two behaviors the suffixed scenario states have no test, so reverting either leaves every test green. (1) `A suffixed task is held to every rule a numbered one is, Done when included`: no test gives a suffixed task no Done when clause (check_task_consistency's done-when check is independent of the number parse, so the claim holds by construction only). (2) `A suffixed heading above the task level in a mixed file reads as a flat remnant, not as a phase`: heading_is_numeric_agrees_with_the_splitter lists no suffixed heading, and no test puts `## 11a.` in a phased file; verified by hand on the built binary that read-tasks omits it and prune-tasks keeps it as structure while dropping the emptied `## Phase 1` (fixture A), so the behavior is right and unpinned. — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:6094` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — test: prune-tasks's `with_line_ending(&counted, line_ending_of(&content))` (prune_tasks.rs:354, with its comment `the file keeps its ending`) has no test. tasks_counter::set creates a new block with `\n`, so removing that line leaves a CRLF file with an LF-only frontmatter block; every prune test uses LF input, and the CRLF case in tasks_counter's tests covers only an existing block. The behavior is right on the built binary (fixture C: a CRLF file gained `---\r\nnext-task: 3\r\n---\r\n\r\n`). — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_tasks.rs:354` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — reuse: tasks_counter::frontmatter_end hand-rolls the opening and closing fence split that `split_frontmatter_with_offset` (mod.rs:1024) provides (`content.len() - body.len()` is the same end offset). Not replaced, because the two differ on a closing fence followed by trailing whitespace (tasks_counter accepts `---` followed by a space, the shared one does not) and on the error (MalformedTasks naming the counter's cause, against UnclosedFrontmatter), and the data-model's malformed-tasks row and the compiled descriptions pin the first. — `runtime/src/primitives/tasks_counter.rs:19` — **waived**: the shared splitter differs in the two ways named, both of which the data model's malformed-tasks row and the descriptions fix for tasks.md; delegating would move behavior a caller reads, and the helper is 15 lines with its own tests (decided by the rule)
- other — prune-tasks reads and replaces tasks.md without append-task's advisory lock (022 task 35, BE-TXN-002), so a prune that read before an append and wrote after it drops the appended task and writes a counter from the stale read. 041's Edge Cases (`Concurrent invocations`) accepts last-writer-wins for a prune, and a feature's pipeline is serial within one agent process; 022's lock covers append-task against append-task. Read `lock_append_task` and the scenario before judging; mark-task, which also replaces tasks.md unlocked, is outside the window's hunks. — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_tasks.rs:297` — **waived**: 041's Edge Cases (Concurrent invocations) states last-writer-wins for a prune as for every primitive that rewrites a spec artifact, and 022's ask-consolidation scenario scopes the lock to append-task against itself; BE-TXN-002 reads design-time commitments about shared persisted state, and the commitment is stated (decided by the rule)
- other — AC21 and the scenario bind `append-task`'s numbering, so after a `--reset` the counter is kept but `/plan`, which the spec's re-derivation contract has the operator run to repopulate, writes `## 1.` onward by hand and so assigns numbers the reset removed. The counter then sits above the new numbers (a gap, never a duplicate for a later append). The scenario's Behavior states `a task number in use before the prune is never assigned to a new task` generally, while its mechanism and AC21 bind only append-task. — `specs/041-task-pruning/scenarios/a-pruned-task-number-is-never-reissued.md:13` — **waived**: AC21 and the owner's 2026-11-12 resolution bind append-task's numbering and rest on the counter, which derives from the artifact; /plan's hand-numbered tasks are outside both, and a reset the operator chose with --reset or --force is the stated destructive path (decided by the rule)
- convention — stale doc comment: the `///` block describing `locate_task_range` (lines 120-124) sits above `compute_skip_mask`'s own doc, so rustdoc attaches both to `compute_skip_mask` and `locate_task_range` has none. Pre-window (b060a823, e20ae91a), in a file whose whole read is this review's. — `runtime/src/primitives/mark_task.rs:120` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention — a ticked criterion, two plan passages and a scenario line state the plan-section routing that `framework/commands/prune.md` no longer follows. AC15 (spec.md:62) says contributor knowledge goes "into `AGENTS.md` when it holds for this project alone"; plan.md:268-270 (Command layer: "`AGENTS.md` for knowledge that holds for this project alone") and plan.md:285 ("`AGENTS.md` for moved knowledge") and scenarios/prune-reduces-the-spec-directory.md:28 say the same. Since ab5eaa49, prune.md step 8 and Scope Boundaries route project-only knowledge by 075's definitions and name `AGENTS.md` only for what every session needs, and prune-walks-the-project-files.md:26 says step 8 "no longer names `AGENTS.md` as its default home". The command follows the newer scenario, so the older criterion, plan lines and scenario line contradict the delivered behavior (plan.md:403 and spec.md:30 already state the new routing). The fix is a criterion edit plus plan and scenario edits inside 041, the parent's to direct. — `specs/041-task-pruning/spec.md:62` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed**

- convention — plan.md's prune-project section omits task 25's design, so it is not the design as it stands. - convention — plan.md's Error taxonomy says of `prune-tasks`' and `prune-plan`'s `feature` argument "The check resolves no symlink, so a link the operator placed inside their own tree is followed." Both now call `validate_feature_argument` (prune_tasks.rs `reduce`, prune_plan.rs `summarize`), which refuses a link on the feature's path, or anywhere under its directory, that resolves outside the project; only a link that stays inside is followed. - convention — data-model.md's `prune-project` result example shows `"search": { "state": "ran", "files-searched": 312 }`, but a ran search always carries `outside-project` and `outside-project-count`; the same file's `search` bullet says so. Run on a fixture (a repository holding one tracked link out and one tracked directory link out), the built binary printed `"search": {"state": "ran", "files-searched": 1, "outside-project": ["src/linked.rs", "vendor"], "outside-project-count": 2}`; with nothing outside it prints `[]` and `0`. — `specs/041-task-pruning/data-model.md:505` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)

- other — a stale comment in `a_linked_spec_root_makes_its_files_outside_project_and_refuses_a_create` says "The refused create is listed after a home the link does not reach, and nothing is written for either." - other — in `label_criteria.rs` the doc comment above `struct Frontmatter` ("Frontmatter block bounds as `(opening_index, closing_index)` over the line vector, or `None` when the file has no leading `---` fence or no closing one") describes `frontmatter_bounds`, the function below it, and names a tuple the code does not use; the struct holds `closing` and `newline`. - other — tasks.md's preamble still says "Phase A ships the `prune-tasks` runtime primitive; Phase B adds the `/{project}:prune` command on top and registers it. - other — a symbolic-link loop at a project file reads `outside-project` though it leaves nothing. `validate_contained` answers InvalidPath for a link it cannot resolve, and `read_subject` and `search` map every InvalidPath to the outside state. The descriptions, data model and prune.md say "resolving outside the project". — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_project.rs:243` — **waived**: the file is neither read nor written either way, and prune.md has the host report it as not examined with its path, so the safe direction holds; a link loop is no file the walk could examine (decided by the rule)
- perf — `search` calls `validate_contained` for every candidate, and each call re-resolves the project root (`canonical_base`) and lstats each path component. On a fixture repository with 20,002 tracked files the whole preview took 0.59 s wall (0.05 s user, 0.54 s system), so the per-file check is not the cost that matters beside the read it guards. — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_project.rs:600` — **waived**: measured on the built binary at 20,002 tracked files; `rewrite_spec_links` already calls the same function per referrer, and hoisting the root's resolution needs `check_links_on`, which is private to primitives/mod.rs (decided by the rule)
- reuse — three sites map `validate_contained`'s InvalidPath to an 'outside the project' outcome and decide the other errors differently: `read_subject` (other errors read `unreadable`), `search` (other errors fail the call) and `rewrite_spec_links` (other errors fail the call). — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_project.rs:243` — **waived**: each site pairs the test with a different outcome and different handling of the other errors, so a shared helper would take a closure per caller for a two-arm match (decided by the rule)
- other — the search's `let Ok(bytes) = std::fs::read(..) else { continue; }` skips a tracked file that cannot be read (deleted from the worktree, unreadable) without naming it, so its identifiers' hits read as 'no literal occurrence found' for a file never read. `outside-project` now names the links; this skip stays silent. — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_project.rs:608` — **waived**: pre-existing in 2f61d67d, which 075's review read; `files-searched` is the count of what was examined that QUAL-CLAIM-001 names as not flagged (decided by the rule)
- bug — a FIFO at one of the four project files hangs `prune-project`: `std::fs::read` blocks on it, with no check that the path is a regular file. Run on a fixture where AGENTS.md was a FIFO, the built binary did not return before a 4 s alarm (exit 142). `rewrite_spec_links` refuses a non-regular file before opening it for the same reason. task 25's containment does not reach it. — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_project.rs:250` — **waived**: contrived (an operator-placed FIFO at a project document in a local single-operator tool), pre-existing, and BE-TIMEOUT-001 leaves local file I/O alone (decided by the rule)
- other — PRUNE_TASKS said the frontmatter block is kept as it was, byte for byte, beside its own clause that the prune gains or updates the block's next-task: line (the fix round's re-read NOTE) — `runtime/src/schema/descriptions.rs` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- contract: the two flattened Option result halves allow neither or both; an untagged enum would keep exactly one — `runtime/src/schema/primitives.rs:2864` — **waived**: rmcp refuses a tool output schema that is not an object, and an untagged enum's root is anyOf; AGENTS.md records the flattened-halves design
- efficiency: the prune-plan walk runs inline on the async worker and reads HEAD per done spec, even for specs it then drops — `runtime/src/mcp/server.rs:710` — **waived**: one HEAD read per done spec is not the unbounded revwalk the blocking pool is scoped to, and a spec it drops still needs the answer to know it can be dropped
- edge: split_frontmatter takes a later bare-LF --- as the fence when the frontmatter closes with CRLF — `runtime/src/primitives/mod.rs:857` — **waived**: no such mixed-ending file exists here, and the fix reaches every frontmatter reader for a case not seen
- contract: an applying prune-tasks walk that errors on a later spec returns no record of the reductions it already wrote — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_tasks.rs:176` — **waived**: each write is atomic and idempotent, so re-running the walk resumes; the data model and plan now say so
- edge: a reset also drops anything above the H1, and its H1 search scans fenced and commented lines — `runtime/src/primitives/prune_tasks.rs:477` — **waived**: a tasks.md opens with its H1 by construction from the template, and no file here has content above it or a heading only inside a fence
- prose: prune.md's opening and step 5 are long, nested sentences — `framework/commands/prune.md:8` — **waived**: readability of correct procedure text; the content is accurate after this run's fixes
- claim: AC11 names only the --reset divergence while the design-record heading set has one too — `specs/041-task-pruning/spec.md:56` — **waived**: AC11 is the tasks.md criterion; the plan's bounded divergence is stated in prune.md, analyze.md and the plan-records scenario
- claim: plan.md narrates the change it makes (gains, is sharpened, is corrected) inside the design record — `specs/041-task-pruning/plan.md:178` — **waived**: a plan describes the change it makes; these sentences state the design, not a history of revisions to it
- reuse: the design-record headings are listed in 041's plan, data model and plan-records scenario beside the template — `specs/041-task-pruning/data-model.md:231` — **waived**: the data model is the contract the drift test holds the runtime constant to, and the scenario records the decision that chose the set
